## Outcome and authority

A genuinely accepted late generator-power mean that can alter an existing Site FindingRevision's `fuel-cost-1` technical input creates durable consequence work atomically with acceptance. Before drain, the old consequence is `STALE/TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED` (or `PENDING` without a head) with a real queued work ID. Draining that **same** FindingRevision yields a current `QUANTITY_UNAVAILABLE/POWER_NOT_IN_ASSESSMENT_INPUTS` revision: the late reading was not an input to its cited analytic run. S3 `process-jobs` and S4 `match-findings` must re-evaluate the Finding and append a revised FindingRevision before a new `CURRENT/PRICED` consequence can become S5-01 eligible. All revisions remain inspectable. A stale read alone does not complete this slice.

Authority: `D-2026-10-13-s4-bounded-financial-basis`, `D-2026-10-14-s402-post-review-follow-ons`, `D-2026-10-14-s5-selected-window-overview`, `.agent/architect-S4-02-batch-answers.md` Q2 and `.agent/architect-contest-2026-10-14d-answers.md` (Planner refinement). This is registry node `I-evidence-acceptance-consequence-work`, separate from S4-02a/b and S5-01's UI checkpoint.

Built anchors: `.ai/CODE_STATE.md` S2-02 and S4-02b phases 1/2a/2b; `PostgresEvidenceLedger.decide_chunk`/`_persist` in `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py` (one chunk transaction; genuine accepted ID returned by `_persist`), `backend/migrations/versions/0011_financial_consequences.py` (work, attempts and triggers), `backend/assetops_backend/financials/{fuel_cost.py,recompute.py,consequence_read.py,adapters/postgres_consequence_store.py}`, `backend/assetops_backend/recompute_consequences.py`, and architecture guard rule 8. Reuse the S4-02b power tools as isolated walkthrough bases without changing their specs. The S4-02b read detects `TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED` for an unchanged FindingRevision, but acceptance currently enqueues no consequence work; its one-shot CLI has no run schedule.

## Acceptance, work and worker contract

1. **Migration 0012 and constraints.** Add `EVIDENCE_ACCEPTED` and a tenant/Site-scoped FK to exact `accepted_evidence.id` on `financial_consequence_work`. An evidence row records accepted ID, Finding ID, selected FindingRevision ID and half-open affected interval. Drop `financial_consequence_work_finding_revision_once`; replace it with a partial unique index on `(finding_revision_id) WHERE cause = 'FINDING_REVISION_APPENDED'`. `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION financial_consequence_dirty_on_finding_revision()` with `ON CONFLICT (finding_revision_id) WHERE cause = 'FINDING_REVISION_APPENDED' DO NOTHING`. Keep assumption uniqueness. Add a partial unique index on `(accepted_evidence_id, finding_revision_id) WHERE cause = 'EVIDENCE_ACCEPTED'`. Replace `financial_consequence_work_cause_shape` with an explicit three-arm CHECK: assumption has only assumption locator; Finding append has only Finding locator; evidence has exact accepted ID plus Finding and revision locators, with other cause locators null. Preserve range and scope checks; index pending selection by tenant/Site and attempt lookup by `(work_id, attempt_no DESC)`. Add nullable typed `no_op_reason` to `financial_consequence_work_attempt`: present only on a `SUCCEEDED` attempt with empty `consequence_revision_ids`; normal success or failure has it null. Keep append-only tables and one-success index. Upgrade preserves 0011 work and bootstrap rows. Downgrade restores the original 0011 trigger body, two-arm CHECK and unique constraint, but **refuses with a clear error if any `EVIDENCE_ACCEPTED` work row exists**; prove both refusal and empty-case downgrade. It never deletes append-only evidence or work.
2. **Transactional enqueue.** Migration 0012 adds an `AFTER INSERT` trigger on `ingestion_disposition` for `decision = 'ACCEPTED'`, joined to its stored `accepted_evidence_id`. It runs inside `decide_chunk` only for a genuine accepted disposition; duplicate, conflict, rejection and exact replay do not enqueue. Filter on the stored, binding-derived `accepted_evidence.role = 'generator_power'`, never a client role. Select existing tenant/Site Findings by strict half-open overlap with their latest revision's episode; zero-width episodes do not overlap. Here and in the worker, **latest eligible FindingRevision** means the Finding's highest `revision_no` in the same tenant/Site; lifecycle/source gating is left to `quantify` and the read, not the selector. Insert one row per accepted ID and selected revision using the evidence partial unique index. Coarse selection may no-op later but must not omit a possibly affected Finding. Trigger failure rolls back acceptance and disposition. Retain the 0011 FindingRevision-insert trigger so new/revised Findings enqueue independently. Before either trigger selects/inserts work, both take the same transaction-scoped Site advisory lock keyed by `financial_consequence_enqueue:` plus `site_id`. At READ COMMITTED, select after acquiring the lock so acceptance racing a FindingRevision append cannot miss its committed revision. Prove with two Postgres connections. Rule 8 remains unchanged: no Python module inserts `financial_consequence_work`.
3. **Scoped drain.** Explicitly dispatch all three causes; an unknown cause records a typed failure (or is skipped) and never falls through to assumption-range recompute, including if phase 1 deploys before phase 2. For `EVIDENCE_ACCEPTED`, queued revision is a locator. Recheck tenant/Site, accepted disposition, bound role/asset, strict interval overlap, Candidate episode/window and latest revision. If queued revision has been superseded, record a typed successful no-op for its work row; the new revision's 0011 insert work derives its consequence. Irrelevant role, asset or window also no-ops without consequence append. A transiently missing/closed source records a typed FAILED attempt and remains retryable; wrong-Site scope fails without fallback. Derive only from that revision's accepted public analytic inputs. The same-revision late reading becomes `QUANTITY_UNAVAILABLE/POWER_NOT_IN_ASSESSMENT_INPUTS` after drain; never substitute it into the old analytic input set. Existing basis uniqueness and per-revision append lock return the existing consequence ID on unchanged/repeated work, concurrent runs and append-then-crash retry. A work item succeeds only when every selected target appended, replayed or no-opped.
4. **Run plan and retry.** Extend the server-side CLI as a **one-shot** bounded `run --tenant ... --site ... --limit N` under an external one-minute timer (document a Windows Task Scheduler or systemd timer invocation, owner, cadence and recovery). Use an explicitly configured tenant and Site; the current development-tenant-only CLI is not an active-Site iterator and must not silently claim production all-Site coverage. For each scoped run, acquire `pg_try_advisory_lock` keyed by `financial_consequence_runner:` plus `site_id` and hold it through selection and processing; if held elsewhere, report the skipped Site. Existing per-item `record_attempt` locks remain. No mutable lease or due-time column is added. Retry eligibility is derived from the last FAILED attempt's `finished_at` and `attempt_no`: use a fixed exponential schedule (1, 2, 4, 8, 16 minutes, then 16 minutes for later failures); there is no terminal cap, and pending work remains visible. Use an injectable clock so due/not-due and later scheduled retry tests do not sleep. One run processes each selected ID at most once and reports selected, appended, replayed, no-op, failed, deferred and backlog counts with Site scope. Price/Finding work uses this runner too. Test two runners, restart, bounded batch, failure, retry and append-then-crash without duplicate basis.
5. **Reads and S5 gate.** Extend `PostgresConsequenceStore.chain()` pending SQL to match unsucceeded `EVIDENCE_ACCEPTED` rows for the selected Finding ID (with revision locator retained for diagnostics), as well as existing Finding and assumption causes. Show queued only when an actual unsucceeded work row touches the selected read; if stale with no work, expose typed `currentness.reasons` code `NO_WORK_RECORDED`, retaining the reviewed `REASONS["STALE"]` sentence. Add no new `REASONS` copy. S5-01 uses money only for a `CURRENT/PRICED` consequence on the same Site, window, FindingRevision and basis after re-evaluation. No new UI copy or panel is authorized here.

## Implementer carries and risks

- C1: Prove a post-0012 FindingRevision append makes exactly one `FINDING_REVISION_APPENDED` row and an `EVIDENCE_ACCEPTED` row coexists for it. Include 0011 bootstrap upgrade and both downgrade paths.
- C2: Pass architecture guard rule 8 unchanged; test that no Python module writes `financial_consequence_work`.
- C3: A reading ending exactly at `episode_start`, one starting exactly at `episode_end`, and a zero-width episode enqueue no evidence work.
- C4: A Site B reading with identical times enqueues nothing for Site A; a forged cross-Site worker locator is refused without fallback.
- C5: Capture exact work, attempt, consequence and `examined_evidence_ids` before/after in the walkthrough artifact, with actual values only.
- R1: S3 `process-jobs` and S4 `match-findings` are one-shot and unscheduled. S4-02c schedules only consequence drain. S5 honestly shows `PENDING`/`STALE` while analytic work is dirty, but current money after late evidence waits for those commands. Architect batch may decide whether their scheduling is a separate W1 follow-on.
- R2: One work row per accepted generator-power ID and overlapping Finding can make a backfill produce hundreds of rows per Site. Test bounded selection/drain against a day of five-minute means across multiple Findings.
- Pre-0012 accepted late power that already left a stale head has no new work backfill in 0012; report `NO_WORK_RECORDED` and leave it for a separately reviewed repair. Do not fabricate a work row for an unknown historic acceptance path.

# Architect rulings: S4-02c review F1 Q1â€“Q3 and R1 (2026-10-14)

Inputs: `.agent/S4-02c-review.md` F1 and registry closeout notes, `.agent/S4-02c-review-packet.md`, `.agent/S4-02c-walkthrough-evidence.md` run 1, `tasks/S4-02c-evidence-consequence-work.md` R1; `D-2026-10-09-s2-simulated-publication-clock`, the S3 dispatch decisions, `D-2026-10-14-s402-post-review-follow-ons`, and `D-2026-10-14-s5-selected-window-overview`. This is a ruling for subsequent work, not a change to S4-02c's reviewed contract.

## Q1 â€” sequence and source time

**Rule:** An admission-allocated gateway sequence is transport/admission order, not proof of source-time order. For a registered same-source, same-epoch stream whose source-time/interval semantics and clock quality are attested and valid, S3 must place readings by source time. A later gateway sequence with an earlier source time is a late arrival, not `SEQUENCE_ORDER_INVERTED` by itself. Preserve duplicate, overlap, conflicting epoch, untrusted clock, and impossible-time refusals; where the source-time basis cannot be established, report a named series limit rather than silently sort and claim coherence. This does not bless arbitrary backdating. The Lab-delayed publication clock supplies a deterministic case: the withheld report has no sequence before release, so admission numbering naturally runs backward against source time.

**Owner and timing:** S3 cohort/assessment (`dispatch-3.x`) plus S2 gateway/publication-profile proof, in `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit`, **before S5-01's money read and before a real-Site pilot**. Changing the reviewed cohort rule requires a new model version, reassessment/reconciliation of affected current windows and preservation of old `dispatch-3.1` revisions and exact links as history. The implementer must prove both on-time-sequenced late upload and admission-sequenced delayed admission through accepted public evidence. A real gateway without validated source-time/clock semantics stays limited.

## Q2 â€” Finding limit and money

**Rule:** A revised Finding must carry the selected assessment's material `GENERATOR_SERIES_AMBIGUOUS`/`SEQUENCE_ORDER_INVERTED` limit when it removes or truncates the prior candidate episode. Its end reason must identify the series refusal; `OPEN_BOUND` alone is false for the run-1 truncation. Preserve the supported `[06:05, 07:00)` portion if its exact evidence remains coherent, but disclose that the later portion is unassessed, not disproved or repaired. S4 owns FindingRevision propagation and its read/UI; a changed claim basis appends a revision.

S5's `mini:4#a` money read **withholds the amount** if generator-series ambiguity affects the selected Finding/window, even if a narrower consequence is `CURRENT PRICED`. Show an explicit no-current-amount state with the assessment/Finding limit and exact links. This is a fail-closed rule for the demonstrated silent understatement, not a claim that every `INDETERMINATE` window has no valid candidate time. Once S3 resolves and reassesses that series under a reviewed rule, S5 may show a same-basis current amount for the proven Candidate span, with any remaining material limits visible. S4 consequence currentness remains a statement about the cited FindingRevision's technical/price basis; S5 eligibility is a stricter presentation gate. Do not rewrite historical $7.5625 or $15.8125 consequences.

**Owner and timing:** S4 Finding projection and S5 `I-site-overview-read`/UI consume the S3 decision in `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit`; **before S5-01**. The real-Site pilot also needs the source-time/clock proof. A regression must replay run 1 and the source-ordered variant and assert the assessment status, Finding limitations/end reason, consequence lineage and S5 amount eligibility; the 7.5625 USD current consequence must never become an unqualified current Overview amount.

## Q3 â€” placement and gates

**Rule:** F1 gets its own sized node, `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` (**M**), because it changes reviewed S3 assessment semantics, S4 claim semantics and S5 money eligibility. It is a hard prerequisite of `I-site-overview-read`, hence S5-01, with `I-S2-02`, `I-S3-01`, `I-S4-01`, `I-S4-02b` as inputs. The node's acceptance target records both late-numbering variants and the no-silent-money result. It also gates the first real-Site pilot. The owner's â€œanalysis input data for simulated and real Sitesâ€ backlog item remains the broader W1-exit contract for per-analytic required inputs, cadence and coverage; it should include source-time/clock attestation at that review, but does not substitute for this narrower S5 defect gate. R1 concerns operating the already defined jobs and is separate from semantic correctness.

## R1 â€” run the S3 and S4 workers

**Rule:** Create `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule` (**S**), requiring S3, S4 and S4-02c's consequence-work node, and gate `I-site-overview-read` on it. Install or explicitly configure an environment-owned bounded schedule for `process-jobs` then `match-findings` across active tenant/Sites; report backlog, last success/failure, retry and stopped-worker conditions. Keep S5 `PENDING`/`STALE_PROJECTION` while upstream work is outstanding. A late accepted reading must reach a revised Finding and consequence without the manual Leg B commands. The S4-02c one-shot consequence runner and its documented external timer do not schedule S3/S4.

**Owner and timing:** S3/S4 worker operation, with S5 currentness integration; **before S5-01**, and therefore before W1 exit or a real-Site pilot that claims automatic late-evidence recovery. The Planner should refine deployment ownership and cadence in a small follow-on packet. No new scheduler mechanism is mandated if an existing reliable environment timer can meet the acceptance target.

## Acceptance and owner decision

S4-02c remains **accepted with carries**. Its transactional enqueue, scoped drain, one-shot run plan and two-leg authored demonstration satisfy its packet. F1 is upstream S3/S4 semantics and a new S5 eligibility requirement; R1 is unscheduled upstream operations. The reviewer explicitly did not make either an S4-02c acceptance gap. Close `I-evidence-acceptance-consequence-work` on merge after the review's documentation carries; leave the two new nodes open.

**Owner input:** No additional product choice is needed to make these safety and lineage rulings. The owner should see the no-amount state and limited Finding at the existing S5-01 visual checkpoint, and decide any broader analysis-input policy at the W1-exit review. If the owner wants partial, explicitly limited money from an indeterminate generator series, that would be a new product/risk decision with a separate reviewed contract; it is not implied by the current S5 eligibility rule.

None implies Healthy, zero cost or no Findings.

4125-S6-01 creates one immutable, tenant/Site-scoped Action identity from a reviewed persisted `FindingRevision` under the canonical Finding route. It pins the originating revision, selected assessment and accepted-evidence/configuration basis; a later Finding revision does not silently rewrite that origin. The recommendation is a bounded proposed reserve-SOC policy trial with explicit purpose, limitation and source Finding; it is not a measured saving or verified outcome. 4126-
4127-Action state is derived from an append-only, server-ordered `ActionEvent` ledger. Record stable event ID, Action and Site, event type, `occurred_at`, server `recorded_at`, idempotency key, actor kind/ID, source channel, authorization basis and triggering command/job or content revision. Idempotent identical retries return the event; conflicting reuse and cross-Site references fail. Historical reads select a recorded cutoff and then `(recorded_at,id)` order; a backdated `occurred_at` is displayed as such but never rewrites the audit order. `PROPOSED`/`ACCEPTED`, application `PENDING`/`APPLIED`/`FAILED`, and later verification status are separate dimensions. Acceptance does not assert application; an applied change or completed work does not assert benefit. A failed application preserves its attempt, error and retry relation without an effective-policy claim. No human author, approval or attestation is inferred from a gateway credential, client field, local OS user or SYSTEM fixture. Under the existing `A-actor-identity` rule, human writes need a verified operator principal; otherwise only a clearly labelled, server-authorized SYSTEM demonstration command with stable service/job provenance is allowed, or the write stays unavailable.

Validate the expected old revision and Site scope under the publish lock; reject stale, unauthorized or invalid edits and record a failed attempt without claiming the new setting took effect. `APPLIED` requires confirmation of the persisted new effective revision; an ActionEvent alone is insufficient. The old basis and every event/change revision remain addressable after reload. Do not mutate a Lab-only override or rewrite accepted evidence. 4114-
4115-`SiteWindowSummary` is a tenant/Site-scoped, selected half-open UTC window read/projection over committed public records. It identifies the exact selected S3 assessment revision and its source/configuration/evidence basis, eligible S4 Finding revisions and any bounded financial consequence revisions, with accepted-source and analytic watermarks, model version, built time, coverage and a typed freshness reason. A user date change selects a new window; reload preserves the requested window and pinned detail IDs. Build atomically from a consistent committed basis or derive an equivalent scoped read; late accepted evidence, analytic/model/configuration changes and consequence work invalidate/rebuild affected windows. A projection is CURRENT only when its accepted-source basis matches the ledger and relevant analytic/consequence work is complete. Preserve old exact revision links when a new result is pending or stale. `UNASSESSED`/Unknown means no completed assessment or insufficient input, `PENDING` means first computation or queued work with no current result, `STALE_PROJECTION` means an earlier result exists but its watermark/basis lags or work is dirty, and `FAILED` names failed work without a usable current result. None implies Healthy, zero cost or no Findings.
4116-
4117-S5-01 presents the selected S3 dispatch objective/status and coverage, an evidence-backed selected-window timeline/operating context, and a bounded optional money card with exact assessment, FindingRevision, consequence/assumption and accepted evidence drill-downs. It must not copy the mock's service, battery, fuel-balance, renewable-opportunity, maintenance allowance, causal charging or recommendation claims without their own source and contract. The money card may show an amount only for a `CURRENT PRICED` consequence on the selected exact FindingRevision, compatible window and current technical/price basis; it labels the `fuel-cost-1` model-conditioned potential exposure with zero lower bound, limitation and demonstration provenance. `UNPRICED`, quantity unavailable, stale, pending, superseded, mixed/unknown source and absent consequence are separate no-amount states. Do not sum overlapping Findings, invent a Site total, or convert this to savings. Ordinary data currently has 0 of 11 qualifying Findings, so show an honest no-current-priced-consequence state and the relevant limiting Finding/Evidence links; the authored P01-power example may show its one amount only with a visible Demonstration-data label and exact lineage. Stored source provenance remains a W1-exit/real-pilot gate, not an implied property of the current date heuristic.

4124-
4125-S6-01 creates one immutable, tenant/Site-scoped Action identity from a reviewed persisted `FindingRevision` under the canonical Finding route. It pins the originating revision, selected assessment and accepted-evidence/configuration basis; a later Finding revision does not silently rewrite that origin. A Finding may have several Actions, but each accepted recommendation creates or idempotently returns one Action for its server-defined command key. The recommendation is a bounded proposed reserve-SOC policy trial with explicit purpose, limitation and source Finding; it is not a measured saving or verified outcome. The Action detail root is `/sites/{site}/actions/{action_id}` with stable child routes and exact `event_id` selection, and Site/Finding back-links. S6-01 may navigate from a known Finding or Action ID before the separate S6-03 Site ActionIndex exists. Hidden unsupported tabs must not masquerade as empty delivered views.
4126-
4127-Action state is derived from an append-only, server-ordered `ActionEvent` ledger. Record stable event ID, Action and Site, event type, `occurred_at`, server `recorded_at`, idempotency key, actor kind/ID, source channel, authorization basis and triggering command/job or content revision. Idempotent identical retries return the event; conflicting reuse and cross-Site references fail. Historical reads select a recorded cutoff and then `(recorded_at,id)` order; a backdated `occurred_at` is displayed as such but never rewrites the audit order. `PROPOSED`/`ACCEPTED`, application `PENDING`/`APPLIED`/`FAILED`, and later verification status are separate dimensions. Acceptance does not assert application; an applied change or completed work does not assert benefit. A failed application preserves its attempt, error and retry relation without an effective-policy claim. No human author, approval or attestation is inferred from a gateway credential, client field, local OS user or SYSTEM fixture. Under the existing `A-actor-identity` rule, human writes need a verified operator principal; otherwise only a clearly labelled, server-authorized SYSTEM demonstration command with stable service/job provenance is allowed, or the write stays unavailable.
4128-
4129-The supported S6-01 change is a controlled reserve-SOC edit through S1 Foundation publish, producing a new immutable effective `FoundationRevision` and an immutable change artifact that links Action, accepted recommendation, authorized command, old/new revision IDs, exact field/value/unit, validity and recorded time, and the application event. Validate the expected old revision and Site scope under the publish lock; reject stale, unauthorized or invalid edits and record a failed attempt without claiming the new setting took effect. `APPLIED` requires confirmation of the persisted new effective revision; an ActionEvent alone is insufficient. The old basis and every event/change revision remain addressable after reload. Do not mutate a Lab-only override or rewrite accepted evidence. S6-01's Execution/History and applied `changes:1#a` show policy activity with event and configuration provenance, including pending/failed cases; they do not show an effect verdict.
4130-
4131-S6-02 owns the separate immutable `VerificationPlanRevision` and later `VerificationOutcome`. A plan pins this Action, the applied-change event and new Foundation revision, a pre-change baseline assessment/evidence basis, target, service/reserve guardrails, required coverage and later-window selection rule before evaluation. Use separate baseline and later Lab runs under their respective fixed effective Foundation revisions; commit their public reports through the ordinary gateway and central acceptance. Evaluate a closed later accepted window against the frozen plan and baseline, with exact analytic/evidence IDs. Missing or conflicting evidence yields `INCONCLUSIVE`; failed guardrail prevents `VERIFIED` even if the target improves; later results append/supersede without rewriting prior outcomes. Plan/evidence/verification child routes preserve exact plan and outcome IDs. `A-S6-verification` remains open for the full comparison rule; this lifecycle decision does not resolve it. S6-03 alone owns the Site-scoped ActionIndex/list and its watermarked empty/positive states; `A-action-index` remains open. Planner must refine S6-01's event transition table, command/API schema, actor path, race/idempotency checks, Foundation publish seam, stored fixtures and desktop/narrow Action-root states against the v6.9 Action screens and technical design before implementation.

# from backend/, with DATABASE_URL set
python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences run --tenant demo --site KOBO-001 --limit 100
python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences pending --tenant demo --site KOBO-001
```

- **Schedule:** an external timer runs it every minute, one task per Site. Windows Task Scheduler:
  `schtasks /Create /TN "AssetOps consequence runner KOBO-001" /SC MINUTE /MO 1 /TR "<venv>\Scripts\python.exe -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences run --tenant demo --site KOBO-001 --limit 100"`
  with "Do not start a new instance" and a start-in directory of `backend/`. systemd: a `Type=oneshot`
  `assetops-consequence-runner@.service` (`ExecStart=<venv>/bin/python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences run --tenant demo --site %i --limit 100`,
  `EnvironmentFile` holding `DATABASE_URL`) and a matching `.timer` with `OnCalendar=*-*-* *:*:00`.
- **Owner:** whoever operates the deployment's backend host.
- **Overlap:** the run takes a session advisory lock `financial_consequence_runner:<site>` on a dedicated
  connection; a second run for the same Site reports `"skipped": true` and exits 0.
- **Recovery:** nothing manual. A failed item records a typed FAILED attempt and stays pending; it is retried
  1, 2, 4, 8, 16 minutes after its last failure, then every 16 minutes, with no cap. A killed run's lock is
  released with its connection; the next tick resumes, and an append-then-crash replays the same consequence ID.
- **Hung runner (alarm):** the run is not bounded by a timeout. A run that hangs (rather than dies) keeps its
  connection and its Site claim, so every later tick prints `"skipped": true` and still exits 0, and "Do not
  start a new instance" hides it further. Repeated `skipped` across consecutive ticks for one Site is the alarm
  signal: find the holding session (`pg_locks` advisory lock on `financial_consequence_runner:<site>`, or the
  stuck process) and end it; its lock is released with its connection and the next tick resumes.
- **Permanent failures (retry volume):** a deterministic failure (for example `EVIDENCE_NOT_ACCEPTED`,
  `EVIDENCE_LOCATOR_NOT_IN_SITE` or a `RECOMPUTE_FAILED` code defect) fails on every retry and appends about 90
  FAILED attempts per day per row, indefinitely; there is no terminal state or alert. Watch `pending` for rows
  whose attempt count keeps growing and repair the cause. Past roughly 1600 such rows per Site at `--limit 100`
  they alone would fill each run's capacity (FIFO-by-eligibility still bounds new work's delay, but the backlog
  grows).
- **Output:** one JSON line per processed item, then a summary line with the Site scope and the `selected`,
  `appended`, `replayed`, `no_op`, `failed`, `deferred` and `backlog` counts. Exit 1 when any item failed.
- S3 `process-jobs` and S4 `match-findings` are not scheduled by this runner (packet risk R1).

"""Operator CLI for the S3-01 dispatch recompute worker (DATABASE_URL names the database).

    python -m assetops_backend.analytics.assess_dispatch process-jobs
    python -m assetops_backend.analytics.assess_dispatch reconcile --tenant-id T --site-id S
    python -m assetops_backend.analytics.assess_dispatch assess --tenant-id T --site-id S \
        --start 2026-10-05T00:00:00Z --end 2026-10-06T00:00:00Z --cohort GATEWAY:SOURCE:EPOCH ...
    python -m assetops_backend.analytics.assess_dispatch match-findings --tenant-id T --site-id S
    python -m assetops_backend.analytics.assess_dispatch finding-rows --tenant-id T --site-id S

Prints one JSON document. `assess` is an explicit cohort choice (persisted under
a cohort-suffixed key); default selection only happens through jobs.
`match-findings` runs the S4-01 `finding-episode-1` matcher over the selected
read; `finding-rows` reloads every stored Finding row of the Site (read-only).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import os
import sys
from datetime import datetime
from uuid import UUID

from assetops_backend.analytics.assessment_inputs import CohortMember, Window
from assetops_backend.analytics.composition import (build_dispatch_assessment_worker, build_finding_matcher,
                                                     build_finding_read_service, build_finding_store)


def _instant(text: str) -> datetime:
    return datetime.fromisoformat(text.replace("Z", "+00:00"))


def _run(run) -> dict:
    return {"run_id": str(run.run_id), "created": run.created, "revision_ids": [str(x) for x in run.revision_ids],
            "new_revision_ids": [str(x) for x in run.new_revision_ids]}


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="assess_dispatch")
    parser.add_argument("command", choices=("process-jobs", "reconcile", "assess", "match-findings", "finding-rows",
                                                "finding-reads"))
    parser.add_argument("--tenant-id")
    parser.add_argument("--site-id")
    parser.add_argument("--start")
    parser.add_argument("--end")
    parser.add_argument("--cohort", nargs="*", default=())
    parser.add_argument("--manifest", default=None, help="finding-reads: case manifest for exact-ID annotations")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    url = os.environ.get("DATABASE_URL")
    if args.command == "match-findings":
        outcome = build_finding_matcher(url).match(UUID(args.tenant_id), UUID(args.site_id))
        json.dump({"planned": outcome.planned, "finding_ids": list(outcome.finding_ids),
                   "revision_ids": list(outcome.revision_ids), "window_statuses": outcome.window_statuses},
                  sys.stdout)
        sys.stdout.write("\n")
        return 0
    if args.command == "finding-rows":
        json.dump(build_finding_store(url).rows(UUID(args.tenant_id), UUID(args.site_id)), sys.stdout, default=str)
        sys.stdout.write("\n")
        return 0
    if args.command == "finding-reads":
        tenant, site = UUID(args.tenant_id), UUID(args.site_id)
        reads = build_finding_read_service(url, args.manifest)
        out = []
        for f in build_finding_store(url).rows(tenant, site)["findings"]:
            body = reads.finding(tenant, site, f["id"])
            out.append({"finding_id": str(f["id"]), "latest_revision_id": str(body["selection"]["latest_revision_id"]),
                        "revision_count": body["revision_page"]["total"], "lifecycle": body["state"]["lifecycle"],
                        "projection": body["state"]["projection"],
                        "recurrence": body["relations"]["recurrence"]["status"],
                        "cited_evidence_count": len(body["revision"]["cited_evidence"]),
                        "annotation_kinds": sorted({a["kind"] for a in body["provenance"]["case_annotations"]})})
        json.dump(out, sys.stdout)
        sys.stdout.write("\n")
        return 0
    worker = build_dispatch_assessment_worker(url)
    if args.command == "process-jobs":
        out = [{"job_id": str(o.job_id), "status": o.status, "error": o.error, "runs": [_run(r) for r in o.runs]}
               for o in worker.run_pending()]
    elif args.command == "reconcile":
        out = [w.payload() for w in worker.reconcile(UUID(args.tenant_id), UUID(args.site_id))]
    else:
        cohort = tuple(CohortMember(UUID(g), UUID(s), e) for g, s, e in (c.split(":", 2) for c in args.cohort))
        out = _run(worker.assess_explicit(UUID(args.tenant_id), UUID(args.site_id),
                                          Window(_instant(args.start), _instant(args.end)), cohort))
    json.dump(out, sys.stdout)
    sys.stdout.write("\n")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
        return self.store.record_runs([out], self.clock())[0]

    # -- jobs -------------------------------------------------------------------
    def run_once(self) -> JobOutcome | None:
        lease = self.store.lease_job(DOMAIN, self.clock(), self.lease, max_attempts=self.max_attempts)
        if lease is None:
            return None
        try:
            computed = [self.compute(lease.tenant_id, lease.site_id, w, lease.knowledge_cutoff)
                        for w in day_windows(lease.window.start, lease.window.end)]
            # A neighbour with no accepted reading in it has nothing to assess (evidence is
            # append-only, so it never had a run either); it stays UNASSESSED.
            outputs = [o for o in computed if o.inputs.cohort.status != "EMPTY"]
            runs = self.store.complete_job(lease, outputs, self.clock())
            return JobOutcome(lease.job_id, "DONE", tuple(runs))
        except LeaseLost as lost:
            return JobOutcome(lease.job_id, "LEASE_LOST", error=str(lost))
        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - every failure is recorded on the job and retried
            reason = f"{type(error).__name__}: {error}"[:500]
            status = self.store.fail_job(lease, reason, self.clock(), max_attempts=self.max_attempts,
                                         backoff=self.backoff)
            return JobOutcome(lease.job_id, status, error=reason)

    def run_pending(self, limit: int = 1000, *, reconcile: bool = True) -> list[JobOutcome]:
        """Each pass first reconciles every assessed Site, so a backdated Foundation/binding
        publication or a model-version change is enqueued without an operator (phase-2 F4)."""
        if reconcile:
            self.reconcile_all()
        outcomes = []
        for _ in range(limit):
            outcome = self.run_once()
            if outcome is None:
                break
            outcomes.append(outcome)
        return outcomes

    def reconcile_all(self) -> list[Window]:
        scheduled: list[Window] = []
        for tenant_id, site_id in self.store.assessed_sites(self.model.ANALYTIC_ID):
            scheduled += self.reconcile(tenant_id, site_id)
        return scheduled

    def reconcile(self, tenant_id: UUID, site_id: UUID) -> list[Window]:
        """Enqueue windows whose latest default run used another model version or a configuration
        the current selection no longer gives (backdated Foundation/binding publication)."""
        now = self.clock()
        scheduled = []
        for head in self.store.run_heads(tenant_id, site_id, self.model.ANALYTIC_ID):
            stale = head.model_version != self.model.MODEL_VERSION
            if not stale:
                config = self.store.configuration(tenant_id, site_id, head.window.widened(BRACKET), now)
                stale = configuration_sha256(config) != head.configuration_sha256
            if stale:
                self.store.enqueue(tenant_id, site_id, DOMAIN, head.window, now)
                scheduled.append(head.window)
        return scheduled
"""Server-side recompute of `fuel-cost-1` consequences (S4-02b phase 2a; S4-02c phase 2 scheduled runner).

    python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences run --tenant demo --site KOBO-001 [--limit 100]
    python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences pending --tenant demo --site KOBO-001

`run` is a one-shot, bounded drain of ONE explicitly named tenant and Site (packet
`tasks/S4-02c-evidence-consequence-work.md` item 4). It takes the Site runner claim
(`pg_try_advisory_lock('financial_consequence_runner:' || site)`) on a dedicated connection; if another runner
holds it, it reports the Site skipped and exits 0. Otherwise it processes at most `--limit` due work items (never
attempted, or past the retry time derived from the last FAILED attempt: 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 minutes, then 16) and
prints one JSON line per item, then one summary line with the Site scope and the selected, appended, replayed,
no-op, failed, deferred and backlog counts. Exit code 0 when every processed item succeeded (or the Site was
skipped), 1 when any failed, 2 for a tenant other than the configured development tenant or an unknown Site,
3 when the store is unavailable. `pending` lists the work still pending with its attempt count and last error.

This CLI is not an active-Site iterator and does not cover every Site of a production tenant: it serves only the
configured development tenant, one Site per invocation. Scheduling (owner: whoever operates the deployment's
backend host; cadence: every minute; recovery: none needed beyond the next tick, because failed work stays
pending and a killed run's claim is released with its connection) is an external timer, for example:

    Windows Task Scheduler (one task per Site; "Run whether user is logged on or not"; repeat every 1 minute;
    "Do not start a new instance" if the previous run is still going):
        schtasks /Create /TN "AssetOps consequence runner KOBO-001" /SC MINUTE /MO 1 /TR
          "C:\\AssetOps\\.venv\\Scripts\\python.exe -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences run
           --tenant demo --site KOBO-001 --limit 100"

    systemd (assetops-consequence-runner@KOBO-001.service with Type=oneshot, EnvironmentFile holding
    DATABASE_URL, ExecStart=/opt/assetops/.venv/bin/python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences run
    --tenant demo --site %i --limit 100; and a .timer with OnCalendar=*-*-* *:*:00, AccuracySec=5s,
    Persistent=false).

An overlapping tick is harmless: the second run finds the Site claimed and reports it skipped.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import sys
from typing import Sequence

from assetops_backend.config import DEVELOPMENT_TENANT_SLUG, database_url
from assetops_backend.financials.composition import (
    CONSEQUENCE_UNAVAILABLE,
    build_assumption_store,
    build_consequence_store,
    build_recompute_worker,
)

DEFAULT_LIMIT = 100


def _limit(value: str) -> int:
    n = int(value)
    if n < 1:
        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError("--limit must be at least 1")
    return n


def main(argv: Sequence[str] | None = None, *, worker=None, store=None, prices=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    run = sub.add_parser("run")
    run.add_argument("--limit", type=_limit, default=DEFAULT_LIMIT)
    pending = sub.add_parser("pending")
    for p in (run, pending):
        p.add_argument("--tenant", required=True)
        p.add_argument("--site", required=True)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    if args.tenant != DEVELOPMENT_TENANT_SLUG:
        print(f"Tenant {args.tenant!r} is not the configured development tenant; this runner serves only that "
              "tenant, one named Site per run, and does not cover other tenants' Sites.", file=sys.stderr)
        return 2
    url = database_url()
    prices = build_assumption_store(url) if prices is None else prices
    try:
        scope = prices.site_scope(args.tenant, args.site)
        if scope is None:
            print(f"No imported Site {args.site!r} in tenant {args.tenant!r}; nothing was recomputed.",
                  file=sys.stderr)
            return 2
        code, tenant_id, site_id = scope
        if args.command == "pending":
            store = build_consequence_store(url) if store is None else store
            for w in store.pending_work(tenant_id, site_id):
                print(json.dumps({"site_code": code, "work_id": str(w["id"]), "cause": w["cause"],
                                  "attempts": w["attempts"]}))
            return 0
        worker = build_recompute_worker(url) if worker is None else worker
        report = worker.run(tenant_id, site_id, args.limit)
        for out in report.outcomes:
            print(json.dumps({"site_code": code, "work_id": str(out.work_id), "cause": out.cause,
                              "succeeded": out.succeeded, "attempt_no": out.attempt_no,
                              "appended": [str(x) for x in out.appended],
                              "replayed": [str(x) for x in out.replayed], "no_op_reason": out.no_op_reason,
                              "error_code": out.error_code, "error_detail": out.error_detail}))
        counts = report.counts()
        print(json.dumps({"site_code": code, "tenant": args.tenant, "site_id": str(site_id),
                          "claimed": report.claimed, "skipped": not report.claimed,
                          "now": report.now.isoformat(), "limit": args.limit, **counts}))
        return 1 if counts["failed"] else 0
    except CONSEQUENCE_UNAVAILABLE as error:
        print(f"The consequence store is unavailable: {error}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 3


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

+# S4-02d — analytic and Finding worker schedule
+
+Status: implementation-ready
+USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED: false, provided this remains an operations/read-contract slice with no new owner-facing copy, panel, or visual state. If implementation changes any of those, reopen the flag and obtain the owner checkpoint. Fresh independent implementation review remains required.
+Milestone: S4 Finding + Financials, prerequisite of S5-01
+Depends on: registry `requires` `I-S3-01`, `I-S4-01`, `I-evidence-acceptance-consequence-work` (resolved). `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` is a separate S5-01 prerequisite, not an input to this schedule.
+Branch when activated: `task/S4-02d-analytic-finding-worker-schedule`
+Planner: Codex, 2026-10-14
+
+## Outcome and authority
+
+An environment-owned, bounded tick runs S3 `process-jobs`, then S4 `match-findings` for every active tenant/Site, then the existing S4-02c consequence drain. A late accepted reading reaches a revised FindingRevision and consequence without an operator invoking the two Leg B commands. Operators can see per-Site backlog, last success/failure, retry and stopped-worker conditions. S5-01 cannot mark a selected window CURRENT while relevant upstream work or an unobserved/stopped worker could leave it behind.
+
+Authority: `.agent/architect-S4-02c-answers.md` R1, `D-2026-10-14-s402c-late-sequence-and-s5-money`, `D-2026-10-14-s5-selected-window-overview`. This packet owns registry node `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule` (S). The separate F1 node owns run-1 sequence numbering and money limitation semantics; this packet does not change S3 cohort rules, Finding limits or price eligibility.
+
+Built anchors: `.ai/CODE_STATE.md` S3-01/S4-01/S4-02c; `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/assess_dispatch.py` (`process-jobs`, `match-findings --tenant-id --site-id`); `analytics/assessment_worker.py` (reconcile, leased jobs and retry); `backend/assetops_backend/recompute_consequences.py` and `financials/recompute.py` (Site claim, attempt-derived retry); `backend/README-db.md` (one-minute external-timer runbook); `tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py` (Leg A/B proof). The current `process-jobs` CLI reconciles all assessed Sites and drains up to 1000 jobs; `match-findings` is a one-shot Site matcher; the consequence CLI serves only the configured development tenant and one named Site. Do not claim those CLIs already cover all active production Sites.
+
+## Schedule, state and S5 contract
+
+1. **Owned Site inventory and cadence.** Configure one deployment-owned timer (Windows Task Scheduler or systemd) at a documented cadence, initially one minute. A server-side orchestrator enumerates active imported tenant/Sites from the authoritative persisted Site registry on every tick, including newly activated Sites; use stable tenant/Site IDs, never a hard-coded demo list or gateway-supplied scope. Define and test the exact active predicate and exclusion of inactive/foreign Sites. Bound Sites per tick, S3 jobs per tick and consequence items per Site, with a durable cursor or fair ordering so a busy Site cannot starve another. The existing S3 worker's global reconcile must still run; expose a bounded drain limit rather than relying on the CLI's default 1000. S4 matching runs only after S3 work relevant to that Site has reached a completed basis; if S3 remains due, leased, failed or deferred, record it as pending and defer that Site's S4/currentness promotion. Match the Site even when no new S3 job exists if a selected assessment/configuration/model basis became dirty. Then drain that Site's consequence work after Finding commits. One tick may leave work pending; later ticks resume it.
+2. **Concurrency and recovery.** Use a database claim for the schedule/tick and per-Site processing, or prove an equivalent cross-host exclusion; a local timer's “do not start another instance” is insufficient. Preserve S3's lease/retry and S4-02c's Site claim/append idempotency. A crash releases or expires the claim, and the next tick resumes without duplicate assessment, FindingRevision or consequence basis. Bound wall time per tick and per Site, including a hung `match-findings`/database call; report timeout as failure and leave work retryable. Use a documented retry/backoff for schedule-level failures, including failed Site discovery, S3 worker exceptions and matcher exceptions; do not silently advance success. Do not add a second independent retry mechanism to S3 or financial work rows.
+3. **Observable durable state.** Persist or derive a tenant/Site-scoped schedule record with last tick start, last success, last failure (stage, typed code and time), running/claim age, retry due, selected/processed/deferred counts and remaining S3/S4/consequence backlog. Define S4 backlog by a durable dirty basis/watermark or equivalent exact comparison between selected assessment revisions and matched Finding basis, not by counting only queued S3 jobs. Expose a read-only operator report/CLI with Site identity and an aggregate; distinguish `RUNNING`, `BACKLOG`, `RETRY_DUE`, `FAILED`, `STOPPED` (missed heartbeat beyond a declared threshold), and healthy idle. A successful zero-work tick advances the heartbeat. Failed and repeatedly skipped/hung ticks cannot look healthy. Carry S4-02c review I1/I2: show permanently failing consequence attempt count/age and growing backlog without capping or deleting attempts; make repeated Site-claim skips and stale in-flight claims an alarm with a concrete recovery procedure. Document the timer identity, credentials, cadence, timeouts, alert threshold, restart and commands in `backend/README-db.md`.
+4. **S5-01 consumption.** Provide a scoped status/watermark read that S5-01 can join to its exact selected half-open Site window: accepted-source watermark, selected S3 run/revision, S4 matched revision/basis and outstanding/failed consequence work plus schedule heartbeat. A first build with outstanding work is `PENDING`; an older exact result with an advanced source/analytic basis or dirty work is `STALE_PROJECTION`; a failed worker with no usable prior result is `FAILED`, with the exact upstream stage/reason available. A stopped or unobserved schedule cannot certify CURRENT, even when a queue query is empty. Only a healthy observed pass after the accepted-source watermark, completed relevant S3 work, matched S4 basis and completed relevant consequence work may permit CURRENT; S5-01 still applies its separate F1 money rule and `CURRENT/PRICED` same-basis check. Keep old IDs and lineage readable. This packet exposes the contract and tests its gate; S5-01 implements the Overview read/UI.
+
+## Phases and focused proof
+
+Each phase is a reviewable result fitting one Claude Implementer context below approximately 120k tokens; obtain an independent fresh review before proceeding.
+
+| Phase | Reviewable result and focused proof |
+| --- | --- |
+| 1. Bounded scheduled orchestration | Persisted active-Site enumeration, fair bounded tick, ordered S3 → S4 → consequence invocation, cross-host claim, timeout/restart behavior and installable timer configuration. Prove two tenants, newly activated Site, inactive/foreign Site refusal, more Sites/jobs than one tick limit, two concurrent timers, crash/restart, idempotent replay and no S4 promotion ahead of due/leased/failed S3 work. |
+| 2. Health and currentness handoff | Durable/derived per-Site stage and backlog report, last success/failure, retry due and stopped/hung alarm; selected-window S5 status/watermark seam. Prove idle heartbeat, failing matcher, failed S3 job, repeated claim skips, timer stopped beyond threshold, permanent consequence failure and recovery. Assert pending/stale/failed versus eligible CURRENT after reload; no queue-empty shortcut. |
+| 3. Automatic late-power walkthrough | Extend or reuse `tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py` in an isolated database. Retain Leg A exact accepted ID, stale queued work, same-revision `POWER_NOT_IN_ASSESSMENT_INPUTS` and history. Release the withheld GOOD mean, then drive only the configured schedule/ticks (no manual `process-jobs` or `match-findings` Leg B calls). Assert the revised FindingRevision cites the late ID; its consequence work reaches the new CURRENT result and passes the bounded S5 eligibility gate only after all upstream work is complete. Record actual run/revision/work/attempt/consequence IDs, watermarks, backlog and heartbeat before/after, replay and stopped-worker state. If run-1 admission numbering triggers F1 ambiguity, assert no S5 amount and use the existing source-ordered variant for the positive amount path; do not fix F1 here. |
+
+Reviewer checks Site inventory and isolation, per-tick limits/fairness, ordering, claim/timeout/retry, restart idempotency, stage-specific errors, heartbeat and backlog truth, currentness under race, and the no-manual-Leg-B artifact. Run focused Postgres worker tests and the isolated walkthrough plus applicable architecture/workflow guards. `USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED` stays false only while the output remains operator/read-contract work; S5-01 retains the owner visual checkpoint.
+
+## Risks and carries
+
+- S3's existing global `reconcile_all()` can be expensive as Sites grow; measure a multi-Site tick and keep bounded scheduling truthful. Do not omit reconcile to meet a timer budget.
+- S4 matching currently scans a Site's selected reads. Large Sites may require a reviewed bounded cursor or dirty-window selection to meet the tick limit; do not mark a partially matched Site caught up.
+- S4-02c retries have no terminal cap: a deterministic failed row can append about 90 attempts/day. Keep the growing count and backlog observable and alertable; do not hide it by reporting only due work.
+- A hung consequence runner can hold a Site claim while later ticks report skipped. Treat repeated skips/claim age as a stopped-progress alarm, with operator recovery; do not count skipped as success.
+- Backdated configuration/model changes can dirty assessed windows without a fresh evidence job. The S3 reconcile and S4 basis comparison must detect them before CURRENT is possible.
+
+Registry comparison: `queue_baseline_nodes` already contains `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule`; keep it there and map its `task` to S4-02d. S5-01's `I-site-overview-read` remains gated on this node and the separate F1 node. No baseline node moves.
+
+Out of scope: F1 late-reading sequence semantics (`I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit`), new cohort or fuel model, new owner UI/copy, S5-01 implementation, real gateway source-time attestation, and a general estate scheduler for unrelated domains.

S5-01 cannot mark a selected window CURRENT while relevant upstream work or an unobserved/stopped worker could leave it behind.
+
+Authority: `.agent/architect-S4-02c-answers.md` R1, `D-2026-10-14-s402c-late-sequence-and-s5-money`, `D-2026-10-14-s5-selected-window-overview`. This packet owns registry node `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule` (S). The separate F1 node owns run-1 sequence numbering and money limitation semantics; this packet does not change S3 cohort rules, Finding limits or price eligibility.
+
+Built anchors: `.ai/CODE_STATE.md` S3-01/S4-01/S4-02c; `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/assess_dispatch.py` (`process-jobs`, `match-findings --tenant-id --site-id`); `analytics/assessment_worker.py` (reconcile, leased jobs and retry); `backend/assetops_backend/recompute_consequences.py` and `financials/recompute.py` (Site claim, attempt-derived retry); `backend/README-db.md` (one-minute external-timer runbook); `tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py` (Leg A/B proof). The current `process-jobs` CLI reconciles all assessed Sites and drains up to 1000 jobs; `match-findings` is a one-shot Site matcher; the consequence CLI serves only the configured development tenant and one named Site. Do not claim those CLIs already cover all active production Sites.
+
+## Schedule, state and S5 contract
+
+1. **Owned Site inventory and cadence.** Configure one deployment-owned timer (Windows Task Scheduler or systemd) at a documented cadence, initially one minute. A server-side orchestrator enumerates active imported tenant/Sites from the authoritative persisted Site registry on every tick, including newly activated Sites; use stable tenant/Site IDs, never a hard-coded demo list or gateway-supplied scope. Define and test the exact active predicate and exclusion of inactive/foreign Sites. Bound Sites per tick, S3 jobs per tick and consequence items per Site, with a durable cursor or fair ordering so a busy Site cannot starve another. The existing S3 worker's global reconcile must still run; expose a bounded drain limit rather than relying on the CLI's default 1000. S4 matching runs only after S3 work relevant to that Site has reached a completed basis; if S3 remains due, leased, failed or deferred, record it as pending and defer that Site's S4/currentness promotion. Match the Site even when no new S3 job exists if a selected assessment/configuration/model basis became dirty. Then drain that Site's consequence work after Finding commits. One tick may leave work pending; later ticks resume it.
+2. **Concurrency and recovery.** Use a database claim for the schedule/tick and per-Site processing, or prove an equivalent cross-host exclusion; a local timer's “do not start another instance” is insufficient. Preserve S3's lease/retry and S4-02c's Site claim/append idempotency. A crash releases or expires the claim, and the next tick resumes without duplicate assessment, FindingRevision or consequence basis. Bound wall time per tick and per Site, including a hung `match-findings`/database call; report timeout as failure and leave work retryable. Use a documented retry/backoff for schedule-level failures, including failed Site discovery, S3 worker exceptions and matcher exceptions; do not silently advance success. Do not add a second independent retry mechanism to S3 or financial work rows.
+3. **Observable durable state.** Persist or derive a tenant/Site-scoped schedule record with last tick start, last success, last failure (stage, typed code and time), running/claim age, retry due, selected/processed/deferred counts and remaining S3/S4/consequence backlog. Define S4 backlog by a durable dirty basis/watermark or equivalent exact comparison between selected assessment revisions and matched Finding basis, not by counting only queued S3 jobs. Expose a read-only operator report/CLI with Site identity and an aggregate; distinguish `RUNNING`, `BACKLOG`, `RETRY_DUE`, `FAILED`, `STOPPED` (missed heartbeat beyond a declared threshold), and healthy idle. A successful zero-work tick advances the heartbeat. Failed and repeatedly skipped/hung ticks cannot look healthy. Carry S4-02c review I1/I2: show permanently failing consequence attempt count/age and growing backlog without capping or deleting attempts; make repeated Site-claim skips and stale in-flight claims an alarm with a concrete recovery procedure. Document the timer identity, credentials, cadence, timeouts, alert threshold, restart and commands in `backend/README-db.md`.
+4. **S5-01 consumption.** Provide a scoped status/watermark read that S5-01 can join to its exact selected half-open Site window: accepted-source watermark, selected S3 run/revision, S4 matched revision/basis and outstanding/failed consequence work plus schedule heartbeat. A first build with outstanding work is `PENDING`; an older exact result with an advanced source/analytic basis or dirty work is `STALE_PROJECTION`; a failed worker with no usable prior result is `FAILED`, with the exact upstream stage/reason available. A stopped or unobserved schedule cannot certify CURRENT, even when a queue query is empty. Only a healthy observed pass after the accepted-source watermark, completed relevant S3 work, matched S4 basis and completed relevant consequence work may permit CURRENT; S5-01 still applies its separate F1 money rule and `CURRENT/PRICED` same-basis check. Keep old IDs and lineage readable. This packet exposes the contract and tests its gate; S5-01 implements the Overview read/UI.
+
+## Phases and focused proof
+
+Each phase is a reviewable result fitting one Claude Implementer context below approximately 120k tokens; obtain an independent fresh review before proceeding.
+
+| Phase | Reviewable result and focused proof |
+| --- | --- |
+| 1. Bounded scheduled orchestration | Persisted active-Site enumeration, fair bounded tick, ordered S3 → S4 → consequence invocation, cross-host claim, timeout/restart behavior and installable timer configuration. Prove two tenants, newly activated Site, inactive/foreign Site refusal, more Sites/jobs than one tick limit, two concurrent timers, crash/restart, idempotent replay and no S4 promotion ahead of due/leased/failed S3 work. |
+| 2. Health and currentness handoff | Durable/derived per-Site stage and backlog report, last success/failure, retry due and stopped/hung alarm; selected-window S5 status/watermark seam. Prove idle heartbeat, failing matcher, failed S3 job, repeated claim skips, timer stopped beyond threshold, permanent consequence failure and recovery. Assert pending/stale/failed versus eligible CURRENT after reload; no queue-empty shortcut. |
+| 3. Automatic late-power walkthrough | Extend or reuse `tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py` in an isolated database. Retain Leg A exact accepted ID, stale queued work, same-revision `POWER_NOT_IN_ASSESSMENT_INPUTS` and history. Release the withheld GOOD mean, then drive only the configured schedule/ticks (no manual `process-jobs` or `match-findings` Leg B calls). Assert the revised FindingRevision cites the late ID; its consequence work reaches the new CURRENT result and passes the bounded S5 eligibility gate only after all upstream work is complete. Record actual run/revision/work/attempt/consequence IDs, watermarks, backlog and heartbeat before/after, replay and stopped-worker state. If run-1 admission numbering triggers F1 ambiguity, assert no S5 amount and use the existing source-ordered variant for the positive amount path; do not fix F1 here. |
+
+Reviewer checks Site inventory and isolation, per-tick limits/fairness, ordering, claim/timeout/retry, restart idempotency, stage-specific errors, heartbeat and backlog truth, currentness under race, and the no-manual-Leg-B artifact. Run focused Postgres worker tests and the isolated walkthrough plus applicable architecture/workflow guards. `USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED` stays false only while the output remains operator/read-contract work; S5-01 retains the owner visual checkpoint.
+
+## Risks and carries
+
+- S3's existing global `reconcile_all()` can be expensive as Sites grow; measure a multi-Site tick and keep bounded scheduling truthful. Do not omit reconcile to meet a timer budget.
+- S4 matching currently scans a Site's selected reads. Large Sites may require a reviewed bounded cursor or dirty-window selection to meet the tick limit; do not mark a partially matched Site caught up.
+- S4-02c retries have no terminal cap: a deterministic failed row can append about 90 attempts/day. Keep the growing count and backlog observable and alertable; do not hide it by reporting only due work.
+- A hung consequence runner can hold a Site claim while later ticks report skipped. Treat repeated skips/claim age as a stopped-progress alarm, with operator recovery; do not count skipped as success.
+- Backdated configuration/model changes can dirty assessed windows without a fresh evidence job. The S3 reconcile and S4 basis comparison must detect them before CURRENT is possible.
+
+Registry comparison: `queue_baseline_nodes` already contains `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule`; keep it there and map its `task` to S4-02d. S5-01's `I-site-overview-read` remains gated on this node and the separate F1 node. No baseline node moves.
+
+Out of scope: F1 late-reading sequence semantics (`I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit`), new cohort or fuel model, new owner UI/copy, S5-01 implementation, real gateway source-time attestation, and a general estate scheduler for unrelated domains.

+| 3. Automatic late-power walkthrough | Extend or reuse `tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py` in an isolated database. Retain Leg A exact accepted ID, stale queued work, same-revision `POWER_NOT_IN_ASSESSMENT_INPUTS` and history. Release the withheld GOOD mean, then drive only the configured schedule/ticks (no manual `process-jobs` or `match-findings` Leg B calls). Assert the revised FindingRevision cites the late ID; its consequence work reaches the new CURRENT result and passes the bounded S5 eligibility gate only after all upstream work is complete. Record actual run/revision/work/attempt/consequence IDs, watermarks, backlog and heartbeat before/after, replay and stopped-worker state. If run-1 admission numbering triggers F1 ambiguity, assert no S5 amount and use the existing source-ordered variant for the positive amount path; do not fix F1 here. |
+
+Reviewer checks Site inventory and isolation, per-tick limits/fairness, ordering, claim/timeout/retry, restart idempotency, stage-specific errors, heartbeat and backlog truth, currentness under race, and the no-manual-Leg-B artifact. Run focused Postgres worker tests and the isolated walkthrough plus applicable architecture/workflow guards. `USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED` stays false only while the output remains operator/read-contract work; S5-01 retains the owner visual checkpoint.
+
+## Risks and carries
+
+- S3's existing global `reconcile_all()` can be expensive as Sites grow; measure a multi-Site tick and keep bounded scheduling truthful. Do not omit reconcile to meet a timer budget.
+- S4 matching currently scans a Site's selected reads. Large Sites may require a reviewed bounded cursor or dirty-window selection to meet the tick limit; do not mark a partially matched Site caught up.
+- S4-02c retries have no terminal cap: a deterministic failed row can append about 90 attempts/day. Keep the growing count and backlog observable and alertable; do not hide it by reporting only due work.
+- A hung consequence runner can hold a Site claim while later ticks report skipped. Treat repeated skips/claim age as a stopped-progress alarm, with operator recovery; do not count skipped as success.
+- Backdated configuration/model changes can dirty assessed windows without a fresh evidence job. The S3 reconcile and S4 basis comparison must detect them before CURRENT is possible.
+
+Registry comparison: `queue_baseline_nodes` already contains `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule`; keep it there and map its `task` to S4-02d. S5-01's `I-site-overview-read` remains gated on this node and the separate F1 node. No baseline node moves.
+
+Out of scope: F1 late-reading sequence semantics (`I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit`), new cohort or fuel model, new owner UI/copy, S5-01 implementation, real gateway source-time attestation, and a general estate scheduler for unrelated domains.

+# S4-02d — analytic and Finding worker schedule
+
+Status: implementation-ready
+USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED: false, provided this remains an operations/read-contract slice with no new owner-facing copy, panel, or visual state. If implementation changes any of those, reopen the flag and obtain the owner checkpoint. Fresh independent implementation review remains required.
+Milestone: S4 Finding + Financials, prerequisite of S5-01
+Depends on: registry `requires` `I-S3-01`, `I-S4-01`, `I-evidence-acceptance-consequence-work` (resolved). `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` is a separate S5-01 prerequisite, not an input to this schedule.
+Branch when activated: `task/S4-02d-analytic-finding-worker-schedule`
+Planner: Codex, 2026-10-14
+
+## Outcome and authority
+
+An environment-owned, bounded tick runs S3 `process-jobs`, then S4 `match-findings` for every active tenant/Site, then the existing S4-02c consequence drain. A late accepted reading reaches a revised FindingRevision and consequence without an operator invoking the two Leg B commands. Operators can see per-Site backlog, last success/failure, retry and stopped-worker conditions. S5-01 cannot mark a selected window CURRENT while relevant upstream work or an unobserved/stopped worker could leave it behind.
+
+Authority: `.agent/architect-S4-02c-answers.md` R1, `D-2026-10-14-s402c-late-sequence-and-s5-money`, `D-2026-10-14-s5-selected-window-overview`. This packet owns registry node `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule` (S). The separate F1 node owns run-1 sequence numbering and money limitation semantics; this packet does not change S3 cohort rules, Finding limits or price eligibility.
+
+Built anchors: `.ai/CODE_STATE.md` S3-01/S4-01/S4-02c; `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/assess_dispatch.py` (`process-jobs`, `match-findings --tenant-id --site-id`); `analytics/assessment_worker.py` (reconcile, leased jobs and retry); `backend/assetops_backend/recompute_consequences.py` and `financials/recompute.py` (Site claim, attempt-derived retry); `backend/README-db.md` (one-minute external-timer runbook); `tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py` (Leg A/B proof). The current `process-jobs` CLI reconciles all assessed Sites and drains up to 1000 jobs; `match-findings` is a one-shot Site matcher; the consequence CLI serves only the configured development tenant and one named Site. Do not claim those CLIs already cover all active production Sites.
+
+## Schedule, state and S5 contract
+
+1. **Owned Site inventory and cadence.** Configure one deployment-owned timer (Windows Task Scheduler or systemd) at a documented cadence, initially one minute. A server-side orchestrator enumerates active imported tenant/Sites from the authoritative persisted Site registry on every tick, including newly activated Sites; use stable tenant/Site IDs, never a hard-coded demo list or gateway-supplied scope. Define and test the exact active predicate and exclusion of inactive/foreign Sites. Bound Sites per tick, S3 jobs per tick and consequence items per Site, with a durable cursor or fair ordering so a busy Site cannot starve another. The existing S3 worker's global reconcile must still run; expose a bounded drain limit rather than relying on the CLI's default 1000. S4 matching runs only after S3 work relevant to that Site has reached a completed basis; if S3 remains due, leased, failed or deferred, record it as pending and defer that Site's S4/currentness promotion. Match the Site even when no new S3 job exists if a selected assessment/configuration/model basis became dirty. Then drain that Site's consequence work after Finding commits. One tick may leave work pending; later ticks resume it.
+2. **Concurrency and recovery.** Use a database claim for the schedule/tick and per-Site processing, or prove an equivalent cross-host exclusion; a local timer's “do not start another instance” is insufficient. Preserve S3's lease/retry and S4-02c's Site claim/append idempotency. A crash releases or expires the claim, and the next tick resumes without duplicate assessment, FindingRevision or consequence basis. Bound wall time per tick and per Site, including a hung `match-findings`/database call; report timeout as failure and leave work retryable. Use a documented retry/backoff for schedule-level failures, including failed Site discovery, S3 worker exceptions and matcher exceptions; do not silently advance success. Do not add a second independent retry mechanism to S3 or financial work rows.
+3. **Observable durable state.** Persist or derive a tenant/Site-scoped schedule record with last tick start, last success, last failure (stage, typed code and time), running/claim age, retry due, selected/processed/deferred counts and remaining S3/S4/consequence backlog. Define S4 backlog by a durable dirty basis/watermark or equivalent exact comparison between selected assessment revisions and matched Finding basis, not by counting only queued S3 jobs. Expose a read-only operator report/CLI with Site identity and an aggregate; distinguish `RUNNING`, `BACKLOG`, `RETRY_DUE`, `FAILED`, `STOPPED` (missed heartbeat beyond a declared threshold), and healthy idle. A successful zero-work tick advances the heartbeat. Failed and repeatedly skipped/hung ticks cannot look healthy. Carry S4-02c review I1/I2: show permanently failing consequence attempt count/age and growing backlog without capping or deleting attempts; make repeated Site-claim skips and stale in-flight claims an alarm with a concrete recovery procedure. Document the timer identity, credentials, cadence, timeouts, alert threshold, restart and commands in `backend/README-db.md`.
+4. **S5-01 consumption.** Provide a scoped status/watermark read that S5-01 can join to its exact selected half-open Site window: accepted-source watermark, selected S3 run/revision, S4 matched revision/basis and outstanding/failed consequence work plus schedule heartbeat. A first build with outstanding work is `PENDING`; an older exact result with an advanced source/analytic basis or dirty work is `STALE_PROJECTION`; a failed worker with no usable prior result is `FAILED`, with the exact upstream stage/reason available. A stopped or unobserved schedule cannot certify CURRENT, even when a queue query is empty. Only a healthy observed pass after the accepted-source watermark, completed relevant S3 work, matched S4 basis and completed relevant consequence work may permit CURRENT; S5-01 still applies its separate F1 money rule and `CURRENT/PRICED` same-basis check. Keep old IDs and lineage readable. This packet exposes the contract and tests its gate; S5-01 implements the Overview read/UI.
+
+## Phases and focused proof
+
+Each phase is a reviewable result fitting one Claude Implementer context below approximately 120k tokens; obtain an independent fresh review before proceeding.
+
+| Phase | Reviewable result and focused proof |
+| --- | --- |
+| 1. Bounded scheduled orchestration | Persisted active-Site enumeration, fair bounded tick, ordered S3 → S4 → consequence invocation, cross-host claim, timeout/restart behavior and installable timer configuration. Prove two tenants, newly activated Site, inactive/foreign Site refusal, more Sites/jobs than one tick limit, two concurrent timers, crash/restart, idempotent replay and no S4 promotion ahead of due/leased/failed S3 work. |
+| 2. Health and currentness handoff | Durable/derived per-Site stage and backlog report, last success/failure, retry due and stopped/hung alarm; selected-window S5 status/watermark seam. Prove idle heartbeat, failing matcher, failed S3 job, repeated claim skips, timer stopped beyond threshold, permanent consequence failure and recovery. Assert pending/stale/failed versus eligible CURRENT after reload; no queue-empty shortcut. |
+| 3. Automatic late-power walkthrough | Extend or reuse `tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py` in an isolated database. Retain Leg A exact accepted ID, stale queued work, same-revision `POWER_NOT_IN_ASSESSMENT_INPUTS` and history. Release the withheld GOOD mean, then drive only the configured schedule/ticks (no manual `process-jobs` or `match-findings` Leg B calls). Assert the revised FindingRevision cites the late ID; its consequence work reaches the new CURRENT result and passes the bounded S5 eligibility gate only after all upstream work is complete. Record actual run/revision/work/attempt/consequence IDs, watermarks, backlog and heartbeat before/after, replay and stopped-worker state. If run-1 admission numbering triggers F1 ambiguity, assert no S5 amount and use the existing source-ordered variant for the positive amount path; do not fix F1 here. |
+
+Reviewer checks Site inventory and isolation, per-tick limits/fairness, ordering, claim/timeout/retry, restart idempotency, stage-specific errors, heartbeat and backlog truth, currentness under race, and the no-manual-Leg-B artifact. Run focused Postgres worker tests and the isolated walkthrough plus applicable architecture/workflow guards. `USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED` stays false only while the output remains operator/read-contract work; S5-01 retains the owner visual checkpoint.
+
+## Risks and carries
+
+- S3's existing global `reconcile_all()` can be expensive as Sites grow; measure a multi-Site tick and keep bounded scheduling truthful. Do not omit reconcile to meet a timer budget.
+- S4 matching currently scans a Site's selected reads. Large Sites may require a reviewed bounded cursor or dirty-window selection to meet the tick limit; do not mark a partially matched Site caught up.
+- S4-02c retries have no terminal cap: a deterministic failed row can append about 90 attempts/day. Keep the growing count and backlog observable and alertable; do not hide it by reporting only due work.
+- A hung consequence runner can hold a Site claim while later ticks report skipped. Treat repeated skips/claim age as a stopped-progress alarm, with operator recovery; do not count skipped as success.
+- Backdated configuration/model changes can dirty assessed windows without a fresh evidence job. The S3 reconcile and S4 basis comparison must detect them before CURRENT is possible.
+
+Registry comparison: `queue_baseline_nodes` already contains `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule`; keep it there and map its `task` to S4-02d. S5-01's `I-site-overview-read` remains gated on this node and the separate F1 node. No baseline node moves.

+Out of scope: F1 late-reading sequence semantics (`I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit`), new cohort or fuel model, new owner UI/copy, S5-01 implementation, real gateway source-time attestation, and a general estate scheduler for unrelated domains.
